# Dynasty Dreams - PRD

## Platform Summary
Multi-sport dynasty fantasy sports platform (NHL active; NFL/NBA/MLB sport-agnostic models ready). Web + React Native mobile, real-time draft room, trade center, waiver wire, matchup simulation, salary cap, commissioner tools, shareable invites (link + QR + SendGrid email).

| 232N.49 (Feb 8, 2026) | **Iter 232N.49 — Draft Chat trade rendering + McDavid chat correction.** User reported two follow-up bugs after 232N.48. **Bug 1 (Trade card empty)**: The Hamilton→Toronto trade card in Draft Chat showed `SENDING: —` and `RECEIVING: —` even though the trade had real assets. Root cause: `DraftChatPanel.tsx` looked for `trade.proposer_assets || trade.from_assets` (SENDING) and `trade.target_assets || trade.to_assets` (RECEIVING), but the actual trade docs saved by the trade builder use `assets_offered` / `assets_requested`. Fix: extended the fallback chains to `... || trade.assets_offered` and `... || trade.assets_requested`. Now the Hamilton→Toronto trade correctly shows `Connor McDavid (C)` sending, `Macklin Celebrini (C)` receiving. Verified via `GET /api/trades?league_id=…` — API payload has the assets under `assets_offered`/`assets_requested`; the fallback picks them up. **Bug 2 (McDavid ghost chat)**: After the 232N.48 hibernation grace undo, the stale "Auto-pick — HC took Connor McDavid" chat message was still visible in Draft Chat, mismatching the Draft Board which correctly showed pick #1 as unassigned. Fix: pulled the stale `autopick-1783475271.475894` message from the draft's `chat_messages` array and pushed a `System` correction message explaining the undo + fresh 4h window. HC's owner now sees the truth of what happened. **Verified live**: Screenshot confirms correction message rendering (`"Auto-pick reverted — HC's pick of Connor McDavid was fired after the server was idle for ~21h..."`) and old McDavid text is gone from DOM. |
| 232N.48 (Feb 8, 2026) | **Iter 232N.48 — Worker hibernation grace + HC pick restoration.** User bug: Hamilton Coyotes was auto-picked Connor McDavid without their input at pick 1 of TFHL Inaugural, and the clock rolled to Toronto. Backend logs proved the root cause: `draft_pick_timer_worker` went **silent for 21.5 hours** (2026-07-07 04:23:16 → 2026-07-08 01:47:51) — the preview K8s container hibernated during a low-traffic window. When the worker woke, it correctly detected HC's 4h clock had elapsed hours earlier and auto-picked immediately. **Fix**: new `_apply_hibernation_grace()` runs BEFORE the auto-pick scan; when `now - last_finished > 3 × interval`, pushes any draft's `current_pick_started_at` forward by the gap for deadlines that fell INSIDE the downtime. **Restored HC's pick**: undone the McDavid auto-pick, current_pick 2→1, clock reset, original pick saved to `draft_undo_audit`. 6 pytests. |
| 232N.47 (Feb 7, 2026) | **Iter 232N.47 — Commissioner "Ping offline manager" button.** Extends the 232N.46 Draft Ops panel. Every offline owner row on an active draft now has a `Ping` button that fires a four-channel nudge: in-app notification, Expo push, SendGrid email, and a real-time Socket.IO `commissioner_ping` event on the target's `user_{id}` room. New backend endpoint `POST /api/leagues/{league_id}/drafts/{draft_id}/ping-owner` (auth: commissioner / co-commissioner / platform admin). Requests validated for (a) target being an actual owner or co-owner of a team in this draft's roster (prevents spam of arbitrary users), (b) draft being active or scheduled (completed drafts rejected 400), (c) rate limit of one ping per (draft, target) per 5 minutes (returns 429 with remaining time). Response includes `channels_fired` array + `next_ping_at` ISO + `cooldown_seconds`. Every ping persists an audit row in `commissioner_pings`. New event `EVT_DRAFT_COMMISSIONER_PING`. Frontend: amber `Ping` button per offline owner on active drafts + sonner warning toast handler on `commissioner_ping` socket event. **5 pytests**. |

| 232N.45 (Feb 7, 2026) | **Iter 232N.45 — Draft engine hardening (auto-start late drafts + defer-to-user offline auto-pick).** User bug: TFHL Inaugural Draft (scheduled 2026-06-26) had never started 10 days later, and Summer Entry Draft was stuck on pick #7 for hours. Root cause analysis found two design gaps: (1) `services/draft_autostart_worker.py` had `MAX_LATE_WINDOW = timedelta(hours=24)` — a scheduling snafu that pushed discovery past 24h wedged the draft forever. Bumped to **30 days** so a real-world late-notice case (misconfigured date, admin on vacation, waiting for teams to join) still auto-boots; ancient junk (>30d) still bounces. (2) `services/draft_pick_timer_worker.py` skipped `missed_pick_action = "defer-to-user"` drafts unconditionally, on the assumption the owner would always be online to click a dialog. Reality: if the manager is offline (or never opens the room), the pick clock ticks for the FULL time_per_pick (e.g. 4h) and the draft stalls. Fix: new `_team_owners_present_in_draft_room(draft_id, team)` helper reads `server.connected_users` (populated on every `join_room` socket event). Worker now defers ONLY when any owner/co-owner of the on-clock team is currently in this draft's Socket.IO room; if all owners are offline AND the clock has expired, we auto-pick with `source="defer_offline_timeout"` for audit clarity. **Live verified**: after backend restart, `draft_autostart_worker` immediately booted TFHL Inaugural Draft to `status=active` with all 24 teams properly seeded (HC on the clock). **Tests**: 5 new pytests in `test_iter232N45_draft_engine_hardening.py` (30-day late boot, defer+offline auto-picks, defer+online still skips, presence helper unit tests). Updated `test_iter230ao` (stale window test now uses 40 days) and `test_iter231q` (defer test now mocks presence=True). **17/17 relevant pytests green**. |
| 232N.44 (Feb 6, 2026) | **Iter 232N.44 — Draft Room performance fixes.** User reported browser slowdowns in `/draft-room/:id`. Profiler + code audit surfaced seven compounding issues; all fixed in one pass. **(1) Timer effect churned every fetch** — `useEffect(..., [draft, timerPaused])` was tearing down + re-creating the `setInterval` every time the `draft` object got a new reference (every 30s poll + every socket event). Replaced with primitive deps `[draftStarted, earlyStart, timerPaused, timePerPick, personalPickSec]` so the interval only re-attaches when actual timer config changes. **(2) Teams re-fetched on every draft refresh** — `fetchDraft()` embedded a nested `/teams?league_id=X` call, so every socket-driven refetch (chat msg, pick, config update) hit the teams API too. Extracted into a dedicated `useEffect([leagueId])` that runs once per league. **(3) Socket callbacks recreated every render** — inline arrow functions in `useDraftSocket(...)` gave the hook a fresh handler object each render (60×/min due to timer). Wrapped all 6 handlers in `useCallback`. **(4) Derived arrays recomputed every render** — `filteredPlayers` / `shortlistedPlayers` / `queuedPlayers` had no memo; on a 50-player list × 60 renders/min that's 9k filter iterations/min. Wrapped in `useMemo`. **(5) `onTheClockTeamObj = teamByAbbr(teams, abbr)`** — O(N) find inline on every render. Wrapped in `useMemo`. **(6) `DraftBoardGrid` team-header render was O(N²)** — for each of 24 team abbrs, `teamByAbbr(teams, abbr)` did a linear scan; wrapped every second by the parent re-render meant 576 scans/sec on a 24-team draft. Replaced with a `useMemo` `Map<abbr, team>` for O(1) lookup, and wrapped the whole component in `React.memo`. **(7) Timer + polling ran while tab was hidden** — added `document.visibilityState` gate to both intervals so they pause on tab-away and resume on tab-return. `DraftPresenceBar` also wrapped in `React.memo`. **Verified**: After landing in Draft Room + 12 seconds of idle, **0** additional API calls (previously would have been multiple teams re-fetches + timer-effect churn). Timer still ticks (135:06 → visible tick), all panels render. |

| 232N.40 (Feb 6, 2026) | **Iter 232N.40 — Draft Board horizontal scroll fix.** Reported: TFHL Inaugural draft (55 rounds) clipped past round 36. Root cause: `ScrollArea` only renders a vertical scrollbar by default (no `orientation="horizontal"` ScrollBar rendered), AND the inner div was pinned to `min-w-[700px]` — for a 55-round draft the columns squeezed together and Radix's viewport clipped the tail. Fix in `lovable/pages/DraftBoard.tsx`: (a) import `ScrollBar` and render `<ScrollBar orientation="horizontal" />` inside the ScrollArea for the pick grid; (b) replace the fixed `min-w-[700px]` with a dynamic inline `minWidth: ${180 + totalRounds * 72}px` so each round column gets a stable 72px minimum regardless of draft size. Verified: all 55 rounds render on Inaugural, scrolling to `scrollLeft=3500` reveals headers Rd 34→55 with pick data intact. |
| 232N.39 (Feb 6, 2026) | **Iter 232N.39 — Draft Board multi-draft rework.** Four user-flagged bugs solved in one pass. **(1) Season-year dropdown** — Label reformatted from `2026 Draft` → `2026/27 Draft Year` via `seasonLabel(y)`. **(2) Clickable draft tiles** — `ScheduledDraftsPanel` is now controlled: parent passes `selectedDraftId` + `onSelectDraft`, tiles gain a cyan ring on selection + "Showing ownership below" / "Click to view pick ownership" hint. First tile (active preferred, else nearest scheduled) auto-selects on load AND when year changes. **(3) Pick-ownership grid + summary badges scope to selected draft** — Summary chips moved directly below the tiles and re-scope on tile click (Summer's 4-round grid → HAM: 4 picks; Inaugural's 55-round grid → HAM: 55 picks). Grid title dynamically reflects the selected draft's name. `handleAddPickToTrade` now stamps the draft_id + draft_name onto the trade payload so a manager can queue picks from Summer Entry AND Inaugural in the same trade builder (Trade Mode: On persists across tile switches). **(4) Pick counts wrong for open teams** — Root cause: `pickCounts` and `yearData` were keyed by team `abbr`, and TFHL has multiple placeholder teams sharing abbreviations (`OS1`/`OS2`/`TBD`). All those teams collapsed onto the same counter — one "OS1" slot showed "40 picks" instead of the correct 4. Refactored to a stable `keyOf(t)` = `id:${t.id}` (falls back to `abbr:${abbr}` for the mock path). Both maps + count accumulator now key by that id-prefixed string. **Backend supporting changes**: `GET /api/drafts/{league}/picks` now accepts `?draft_id=X` filter, and its `to_list()` cap raised 1,000 → 10,000 (a 24×55 draft alone is 1,320 rows and was silently truncated). **Season-year filter** in `ScheduledDraftsPanel` uses hockey-season boundaries (`Jun 1 YYYY` → `Jun 30 YYYY+1`) so a Winter draft scheduled Jan 2027 correctly appears under "2026/27 Draft Year". Verified: all 3 TFHL drafts appear as tiles; clicking each swaps the grid + summary; every team shows the correct number of picks (4 for Summer, 55 for Inaugural). ESLint + ruff green. |

| 232N.33 (Feb 6, 2026) | **Iter 232N.33 — Draft timer sync fix + per-draft-instance Draft Board panel.** User bug (chriszarek@gmail.com): "The draft timer only ticks on page load — sitting on the page freezes the clock. And the Draft Board groups everything under 2026 as '0 picks' when there should be one board per scheduled draft (Summer Entry, Winter Entry, Inaugural)." **Timer fix (`DraftRoomPage.jsx`)** — `fetchDraft` used to hard-reset `pickTimeLeft` to `draft.time_per_pick` on every mount, so a manager who opened the war room 3h into a 4h pick clock saw "4:00:00 left" instead of the true "1:00:00 left". Now computes `elapsed = (now - current_pick_started_at) / 1000` and initialises `pickTimeLeft = max(0, base_seconds - elapsed)` on both the main clock AND the personal timer. The existing `setInterval` tick loop was already correct — it just needed the correct starting value. **Draft Board panel (`DraftBoard.tsx`)** — new `ScheduledDraftsPanel` component slotted into the Pick Ownership tab. Fetches `GET /api/drafts?league_id=X`, filters to `status ∈ {scheduled, active, completed}`, sorts active→scheduled→completed and by `scheduled_time` ASC within each bucket. Renders one gradient-card per draft with: title, formatted `scheduled_time`, `total_rounds`, teams count, current picks count (`picks.length / total_rounds × teams`), status badge (amber/emerald/cyan colour-coded), and a status-aware action link (Open draft room for active, View results for completed, "Scheduled — waiting to start" for scheduled). Empty state: "No drafts scheduled yet — commissioners can create one from League Admin → Draft." **Verification (testing agent iter 145)** — 3/3 bugs verified FIXED (100%): (a) panel + 3 cards rendered for TFHL (Summer Entry ACTIVE 3/96, Inaugural SCHEDULED 0/1320, Winter SCHEDULED 0/55); (b) timer ticked 5s in 5s wall-time (223:22 → 223:17); (c) reload persistence — 1st reload 223:13, 2nd reload after 3s sleep = 223:06, confirming the timer computes from `current_pick_started_at` rather than resetting on mount. Non-blocking observation: WebSocket 403 handshake in preview env (pre-existing infra concern, does not affect the polling-based timer). |
| 232N.32 (Feb 3, 2026) | **Iter 232N.32 — "Smart-arranged for you" toast.** User asked for a one-time transparency nudge showing WHICH widget moved and WHY when the first-run smart-order fires. **Hook** — `applySmartOrder(signals)` now returns `{arranged, topWidget, previousTopWidget, topScore, signals}` when the sort meaningfully changed the leading widget (returns `null` when the top widget was already at position 0 → no toast for edge cases where the default already matched the data). **Frontend** — the LeagueDetailPage smart-arrange effect consumes that return value. When non-null, it maps `topWidget` to a friendly label (`LABELS`) + a reason phrase (`REASONS`) driven by the actual counts we already have in state (e.g. `pending_trades → "3 trades need your review"`, `top_trade_block → "8 players on the block"`, `matchups → "Your live matchup is here"`). Fires a sonner `toast.success("Smart-arranged for you", { description, duration: 5000, action: {label: "Reset", onClick: () => resetWidgets()} })` so the manager gets a one-tap escape hatch back to defaults. **UX detail** — the Reset action ties directly into `useLeagueHomeConfig.reset`, which re-arms `first_run_completed: false` on the server so the next visit will re-run the smart-arrange with fresh signals (perfect for the "I'm coming back to a slow week and want a fresh relevance sort" case). **Verification** — smoke-tested on chriszarek's Frozen Dynasty: after wiping the layout, the toast rendered in the top-right showing "Smart-arranged for you | Pending trades moved to the top · 3 trades need your review | Reset" as expected. |

Copy: title = `"+25 XP"`, description = `"Trade accepted · Line Captain in 3"` (source label + level title + xp_to_next_level). Uses dynamic `import('sonner')` so routes that never see the event pay zero overhead. **Tests** — new `backend/tests/test_iter232N24_xp_helper.py` (8 tests, all pass): level_from_xp bench-warmer (0-149 → level 1), ramp-up (350 XP → level 3), level-6 title flip (Rink Regular at 1250 XP), XP_DELTAS constants locked to users.py formula, emit_xp_gain broadcasts to `user_{id}` room with correct payload shape, no broadcast_fn = no-op, unknown source = no-op, compute_user_xp returns 0 for empty user. | 232N.23 (Feb 3, 2026) | **Iter 232N.23 — MY LEAGUES widget movement fix (universal drag across users).** User bug: "Widget movement for 'my leagues' isn't there [for chriszarek]. Please fix so user dashboard functionality is universal across all users." **Root cause** — `MyLeaguesOrderedList` had its OWN HTML5-native `draggable` row primitive (each league row = a drag source for internal reorder), plus a `SwipeableRow` gesture wrapper. When users tried to grab the MY LEAGUES card to reorder the widget, the row-level `draggable` intercepted the pointer event first and dnd-kit's section-drag sensor never activated. Chris was affected more visibly than admin because he has 7 leagues (many rows, each intercepting drags); admin has 4 with more empty space. **Fix** — `SortableSections.jsx` exports a new `useSortableCustomize()` hook consuming the existing `SortableCtx`. `MyLeaguesOrderedList` reads that hook and, when customize mode is ON: (a) sets `draggable={false}` on every row, (b) drops all `onDragStart/Over/Leave/Drop/End` handlers so the row can't intercept, (c) bypasses the `SwipeableRow` wrapper entirely (renders the row body directly), (d) switches the italic hint text from "Drag to reorder • your preference is saved locally" to "Customize mode — drag the whole card to reorder widgets" so users understand which gesture is active, (e) suppresses the swipe onboarding pulse. Once Customize mode is toggled OFF, all the internal drag + swipe + hint behaviour returns instantly. Applies to every user identically — chris + admin verified. **Dead-code cleanup** — dropped the now-unused `stats.teams` field + duplicate `/users/me/week-at-a-glance` fetch from `DashboardPage.jsx` (flagged by testing agent iter 140). MyWeekAtAGlance widget owns its own fetch so the page-level call was pure overhead. **Verification (testing agent iter 141)**: 100% frontend — chris drag DOM order flipped `[pending-trades, week-at-a-glance, my-leagues, ...]` → `[pending-trades, my-leagues, week-at-a-glance, ...]` after grabbing the section handle; admin identical behaviour; exit-customize restores row-drag + swipe. Chris's saved layout auto-restored via PATCH `/users/me/dashboard-layout`. |

| 232N.21 (Feb 3, 2026) | **Iter 232N.21 — Dashboard drag ergonomics fix + Fantasy Level badge mobile fit.** User: "The ability to move widgets in the user dashboard doesn't seem to be working, please review and fix. 'Customize layout' button highlights the widget but it doesn't seem to move despite attempts to. The player badge with stats should fit across the mobile screen. Currently not aligned nicely as a result of the size." **Drag fix — loosened activation constraints in `SortableSections.jsx`.** The PointerSensor was configured with `activationConstraint: { distance: 6 }` (mouse must move 6px WHILE held before drag activates); TouchSensor had `{ delay: 200, tolerance: 5 }` (finger must hold still for 200ms). Both were rejecting natural drag gestures — a user who tapped-and-immediately-dragged on mobile got their gesture interpreted as a scroll before the 200ms delay elapsed. Now: PointerSensor `{ distance: 4 }` (any drag ≥ 4px triggers) + TouchSensor `{ distance: 5 }` (no delay — matches PointerSensor behaviour on touch). Also added inline `style={{ touchAction: "none" }}` on the drag-handle button so mobile browsers stop hijacking the first ~10px of finger movement for their built-in vertical scroll gesture before dnd-kit's sensor gets a chance to see it. Verified live at 390×844 — a natural drag (no hold) now reorders widgets. Applies to every page that uses the primitive (dashboard + League Home). **Badge mobile fit — `FantasyLevelBadge.jsx` refactored.** Wrapped the whole card in tighter mobile paddings (`p-3 sm:p-4`), forced the Fantasy Level header row to `min-w-0` + `truncate` so the "Rink Regular" title pill sits inline with the icon instead of wrapping, added a `max-w-[55%]` clamp on the title pill so it can't push the icon off-screen on 320px devices, dropped the level number from `text-4xl` to `text-3xl sm:text-4xl` and the XP subline to `text-[11px] sm:text-xs`, and compressed the 3-column stat grid to `gap-1.5`, `text-[9px]` labels, and `text-sm` stat values on mobile with `min-w-0` + `truncate` on every cell so the row lays out cleanly at 320px. Card now renders at 221×157 on mobile (was 221×220 with elements overflowing the pill). Also added `tabular-nums` on the stat counts so "0/0/0" and "144/12/8" both align. No functional change — pure layout. |

| 232N.12 (Feb 3, 2026) | **Iter 232N.12 — Chat channel dedup (DMs + default channels) + atomic upserts + unique indexes + sidebar label fix.** User bug reports: "In Chat, why would there be multiple direct messages to one user? It should be one DM thread." + "Waivers and Players have disappeared from the Team Management sidebar." **Root cause (chat)** — `_ensure_dm_channel` and `_ensure_default_channels` both used the non-atomic `find_one() → insert_one()` pattern. When two participants opened `/chat/channels` simultaneously, both `find_one` calls returned None and both proceeded to insert, producing a duplicate DM row (same for default channels — `general`/`trades`/`trash-talk` were being created N times per league). Live TFHL data had 82 DM channels across only 58 unique team-pairs (24 dupes) plus doubled `#general`/`#trades`/`#trash-talk` in every league Chris was in. **Backend** — (a) `_ensure_dm_channel` now uses `find_one_and_update(upsert=True, return_document=AFTER)` on a scalar `dm_pair_key` (sorted `"teamA|teamB"`). Can't index on the raw `team_pair` array — Mongo indexes each array element individually so two DMs sharing a team collide on that team's id. (b) `_ensure_default_channels` now atomic-upserts once per default channel name. (c) New startup migration `dedupe_dm_channels_and_create_index(db)` runs in `server.py:on_startup`: aggregates `(league_id, team_pair)` groups with count>1, picks the oldest survivor, re-parents `chat_messages` / `chat_reads` / `chat_mutes` onto it, deletes the newer duplicates, backfills `dm_pair_key` on every remaining DM, does the same merge for default channels grouped by `(league_id, name)`, then creates partial unique indexes `uniq_dm_pair_key_per_league` and `uniq_default_channel_per_league`. Idempotent — safe to re-run every boot. Live result on this DB: `pairs_merged=40, channels_deleted=40, keys_backfilled=106` (37 DM + 3 default-channel dupe cohorts). Both unique indexes now enforce dedup at the storage layer, so future races are rejected even if the app-level upsert regresses. **Frontend** — sidebar `Team Management` entry renamed from "Free Agents" back to **"Free Agents / Waivers"** so managers used to the legacy "Waivers / Players" label still recognise it after the URL migration to `/players`. Pending-claim badge lookup updated to match. **Tests** — new `backend/tests/test_iter232N12_chat_dedup.py` (3 tests, all pass): (1) 16 concurrent `_ensure_dm_channel` calls (both team orderings) produce exactly ONE DM row; (2) 6 concurrent `_ensure_default_channels` calls produce exactly 3 default channels; (3) migration merges seeded duplicates, keeps the older survivor, and re-parents messages to it. Full iter232N cluster regression pass: **17/17 green**. |

Managers who forgot about a claim would see it in their face as the clear-time approaches." **Backend** — `routes/players.py:get_players` gains a new `waivers_first: bool = False` query param. When ON + `league_id` is provided + the league has at least one pending claim, the endpoint drops out of the plain `find().sort()` path into an aggregation pipeline: pre-sorts the waiver pids by soonest `processes_at` ascending, uses `$indexOfArray` inside `$addFields` to stamp a `_waiver_rank` on every doc (position within the waiver queue, or a `1e9` sentinel for non-waiver rows), then `$sort {_waiver_rank: 1, <primary_sort>: <direction>}`, then `$skip`/`$limit` for pagination. Net effect: waiver-clearing players surface at the top in soonest-clear order, and everyone else falls through to the caller's usual sort behind them. Silent no-op when there are zero pending claims or no league context — keeps the toggle safe to leave on. Note: Mongo `$let` variable names cannot start with underscore (`_idx` → `idx`), a subtle constraint the first cut missed. **Frontend** — new "Waivers first" chip in `PlayersPage.jsx` toggles alongside "Undrafted only" / "Show my players" in the Available view (amber styling to match the "W" pill). Only rendered in the `available` view (has no meaning elsewhere). **Tests** — new `backend/tests/test_iter232N11_waivers_first_sort.py` (3 tests, all pass): (1) with 2 low-point waiver players (staggered `processes_at`) + 2 high-point FAs, OFF sorts high-point FAs first (default), ON puts WEarly (6h) then WLate (48h) ahead of Hi1/Hi2 — `waiver_status` still stamped correctly regardless of sort; (2) toggle is a no-op when the league has zero pending claims — response matches the default order exactly; (3) toggle is silently ignored when no `league_id` is provided (doesn't 500). Live curl on TFHL with 2 seeded pending claims confirms Michael Misa (clears 03:08 UTC) + Ryan McDonagh (clears 09:08 UTC) surface at the top, ahead of Connor McDavid (138 pts) — exactly the intended UX. | 232N.3 (Feb 2, 2026) | **Iter 232N.3 — Websocket status-change toasts + PlayerRow data-testid.** User: "Emit a websocket event when a player's flag changes (e.g. moved to IR, called up from AHL, signed a contract). Emit templates cover both directions: injured on/off ("Injury: {name}" / "Cleared: {name}"), minors on/off ("Sent down" / "Called up"), unsigned on/off ("Contract lapsed" / "Signed"). Added admin-only endpoint `POST /api/players/sync-status-flags?league_id={id}` (routes/players.py) that unions every roster in the league, then runs the detector — safe to run repeatedly, idempotent by design. Simplifies the compute (no more roster diff) and re-frames the badge as "fresh name to the league" rather than "we've been here before". **Multi-badge support** — already worked; `PlayerStatusBadges` correctly filters all true flags and renders one icon per active flag, so an injured+unsigned+undrafted prospect shows 3 badges side-by-side. **Backend** — `routes/draft_inspection.py:get_draft_eligible_players` now computes `previously_drafted_pids` (all prior-draft picks in the league) and stamps `status_flags["undrafted"] = pid_str NOT in previously_drafted_pids` (guarded on `league_id_str` being truthy so we don't misleadingly flag `undrafted=True` in edge cases). Dropped the roster-diff `previously_drafted_dropped` computation entirely. **Frontend** — `components/player/PlayerStatusBadges.tsx` renames the interface key + BADGE_META entry: `undrafted` uses the `Sparkles` icon in emerald with tooltip "Undrafted in this fantasy league — never picked before". **Tests** — updated `test_iter232N_pick_league_and_status_flags.py` (5 tests, all pass): now asserts previously-drafted players get `undrafted: false` while every never-drafted player gets `undrafted: true`, and the roster-status guard test is renamed accordingly. Live curl on TFHL Summer Entry Draft: 1,338 eligible prospects with correct amateur-league labels (NCAA/RUSSIA-JR./USHL/…) and every one carrying `undrafted: true`. Multi-badge case verified live: Alex Pharand (OHL) with mock flags returns `{injured:true, in_minors:false, unsigned:true, undrafted:true}` — 3 badges concurrent. |

| 232M.4 (Mar 1, 2026) | **Iter 232M.4 — Per-thread mute toggle.** User: "A per-thread mute toggle (small bell icon in the TradeThreadTimeline header — 'Mute this negotiation') would let managers who've already tapped out of a long counter chain silence just that thread without silencing all trade notifications league-wide. Piggybacks on the existing `conditional_subscriptions` collection pattern." **Backend** — new collection `trade_thread_mutes` with `{thread_root_id, user_id, muted_at}`. Two new endpoints: `GET /api/trades/{trade_id}/thread/mute-state` returns `{thread_root_id, muted}` for the calling user; `POST /api/trades/{trade_id}/thread/mute` with body `{muted: bool}` upserts (mute=true) or deletes (mute=false) the row. Both endpoints resolve the thread root via `_resolve_thread_root_id(db, trade_id)` so managers can mute from ANY step in the chain and the mute applies to the entire thread. `_notify_thread_participants` (iter 232M.3) now filters muted users out of the fanout list via a single `find({thread_root_id, user_id: {$in: recipients}})` round-trip — added directly before the create_notification loop, so both accept + reject + counter fanouts respect mutes. **Frontend** — `TradeThreadTimeline` now fetches the mute state in parallel with the thread on mount and renders a **Bell / BellOff toggle button** in the header (data-testid `trade-thread-mute-toggle`) next to "Negotiation History · N steps". Clicking optimistically flips the state and posts to the toggle endpoint; failures roll back with a toast. Muted state shows "Muted" label + secondary bg, unmuted shows "Mute" in sky-300. Toast confirms transitions ("Notifications muted for this negotiation" / "Notifications on for this negotiation"). **Tests** — new `backend/tests/test_iter232M4_thread_mute.py`, 2 tests: (1) `test_mute_toggle_persists_by_thread_root` — mute via a counter step and verify mute-state reads True from BOTH the root and the counter (mute is keyed on root, not step). Then unmute and confirm. (2) `test_fanout_skips_muted_users` — bystander mutes, target rejects, assert bystander is NOT in the notify list while the original proposer still is. 5/5 iter232M.3+M.4 tests pass. Full iter232M cluster: 28/28 including regressions. Live curl round-trip on TFHL executed trade: initial→muted→state=muted→unmuted, all clean. |
| 232M.3 (Mar 1, 2026) | **Iter 232M.3 — Thread-wide fanout notifications.** User: "Add a notification when a counter you're party to gets accepted/rejected/countered further — right now only the direct recipient of the new counter is pinged. Piggybacking on the thread lookup, we could notify EVERY unique proposer/target across the chain so no negotiation participant misses the outcome." **Implementation** — new helper `_notify_thread_participants(db, trade, *, title, body, league_id, link, exclude_user_ids)` in `routes/trades.py`. Resolves the thread root via `counter_thread_root` (or `_id` when trade IS the root), queries `{$or: [{_id: rootId}, {counter_thread_root: rootId}]}` (same pattern the thread timeline endpoint uses so behaviour stays consistent), collects the union of `proposer_id` and `target_owner_id` from every step in the chain, subtracts the exclude set, and issues `create_notification` for the rest. Best-effort — per-recipient failures are logged and swallowed so one dead user id can't block the response path. **Wired into all three respond branches**: (a) `accept` → title "Counter Accepted — Pending Review", body mentions review window and links to the accepted trade; (b) `counter` → title "Counter Chain Updated", body describes another counter arrived and links to the new counter; (c) `reject` → title "Counter Chain Rejected", body notes the deal collapsed with the optional reject reason echoed in. In every case the exclude set includes the acting user + the primary recipient of the direct notification (typically the original proposer of the step being acted on) so nobody receives duplicates. Uses the existing `NT_TRADE_PROPOSED` notification type so existing preference toggles / mute rules apply automatically. **Tests** — new `backend/tests/test_iter232M3_thread_fanout.py`, 3 tests: (1) `test_counter_fans_out_to_thread_participants` seeds a chain with a synthetic bystander step and asserts the bystander gets fanned in on counter, while the acting user is excluded. (2) `test_reject_fans_out_to_thread_participants` — same for reject action. (3) `test_accept_fans_out_to_thread_participants` — same for accept action. All 3 pass. Full iter232M cluster (26 tests) + iter231h regressions still green. |
| 232M.2 (Mar 1, 2026) | **Iter 232M.2 — Trade thread timeline.** User: "Since the counter flow is now round-trippable, a thread timeline component on /transactions/trade/{id} — 'Proposal #1 → Counter #2 → Counter #3 → Accepted', using the `counter_thread_root` + `counter_depth` fields I already store — would give managers a full negotiation history in one view." **Backend** — new `GET /api/trades/{trade_id}/thread` endpoint in `routes/trades.py`. Resolves the thread root (self, or `counter_thread_root` when this trade is itself a counter), queries `{$or: [{_id: rootId}, {counter_thread_root: rootId}]}`, and returns the entire chain sorted by `counter_depth`. Payload per step is intentionally slim (id / status / counter_depth / proposer & target names + ids / submitted_at / responded_at / processed_at / response_action / message) — no asset hydration, so this is cheap even for long threads. Returns `{root_id, current_id, count, thread}`. **Frontend** — new `TradeThreadTimeline` component (`components/trade/TradeThreadTimeline.tsx`) fetches the thread on mount and renders a vertical timeline with a connector rail: each step gets a **kind chip** ("Proposal" or "Counter #N"), the two-team direction chip (`Prop → Targ`), a status pill (icon + label matching the trade statusConfig — Accepted/Executed emerald, Rejected/Vetoed rose, Countered sky, Pending/Proposed amber/cyan, Cancelled/Withdrawn zinc), timestamps, and an "Open →" cross-link for the non-viewed steps. The **currently-viewed** step is highlighted with a sky-500 ring + a "VIEWING" marker chip so managers immediately know where they are. Optional message from each step renders as an italic quote. Component **no-ops when `thread.length <= 1`** so plain trades stay clean — the timeline only appears once there's been at least one counter. Replaces the two "View counter →" / "View original →" cross-link banners from iter 232M (now redundant — timeline shows both directions inline). New data-testids: `trade-thread-timeline`, `trade-thread-step-{idx}`, `trade-thread-current-marker-{idx}`, `trade-thread-step-link-{idx}`. **Tests** — new `backend/tests/test_iter232M2_thread_timeline.py`: (1) `test_thread_returns_full_chain_in_depth_order` seeds a real Root → Counter #1 → Counter #2 chain via the /respond endpoint (so `counter_thread_root` + `counter_depth` get stamped exactly as production does), then asserts fetching /thread from ANY of the 3 entry points returns the same 3-step chain sorted by depth with the correct swap in proposer/target names, and the root+counter#1 statuses flip to `countered` after each next counter. (2) `test_thread_for_uncontested_trade_returns_single_entry` verifies plain trades return count=1 so the timeline stays hidden. 7/7 iter232M cluster tests pass. Live verification: `GET /trades/6a3f5357e16497918ebf4d0f/thread` returns `{count: 1}` for the existing standalone TFHL executed trade — timeline correctly stays hidden. |

| 232M (Mar 1, 2026) | **Iter 232M — Counter offers + lock-aware trade execution.** User (after confirming the trade lifecycle audit): "a + b" — both features. **(a) Counter action.** `POST /trades/{id}/respond` now accepts `action=counter` alongside `accept`/`reject`. On counter: (1) validate at least one asset on either side of the counter, (2) hydrate player details, (3) INSERT a new trade doc going the other way (proposer = original target, target = original proposer) with `status=proposed`, `countered_from=<original_id>`, `counter_thread_root=<root_id>` (chain of counters can be walked), and `counter_depth+1`, (4) mark the ORIGINAL trade `status=countered` (terminal) with `countered_by_trade_id=<new_id>`, `response_action=counter`. Broadcast `trade_countered` and notify the original proposer with a deep-link to the new counter. Frontend: `statusConfig.countered` = Reply icon / sky-500 pill; `TradeDetail.tsx` adds a **Counter** button (data-testid `trade-detail-counter-btn`) that navigates to `/trade-block?counter_from={id}&with={original_proposer_team_id}` for asset selection; countered-thread cross-link banners (data-testids `trade-detail-countered-by-link` + `trade-detail-countered-from-link`) let managers walk the thread in both directions; `STATUS_META.countered` in TradeDetail. **(b) Defer during matchup lock.** `services/trade_engine.py::_execute_trade` now consults `utils/lineup_lock.evaluate_lineup_lock(league)` before swapping rosters. When locked, it stamps `defer_reason="matchup_locked"` + `defer_next_unlock=<iso>` + `deferred_at` on the trade doc and returns `{"executed": False, "deferred": True, "reason": "matchup_locked"}` WITHOUT running the swap. On the next call after the lock lifts, the defer flags are $unset. Commissioner overrides via `trade.force_execute = True` bypass the guard. Added a **pending-trade auto-processor** background sweeper in `server.py` (60s cadence) that finds `status=pending, processes_at <= now` and calls `_execute_trade` — deferred trades stay pending and retry on the next tick, idempotently. Frontend: `statusConfig.DEFERRED_PILL` (Lock icon / purple-500), rendered on TradeCard alongside the status badge when `deferReason==="matchup_locked"` (data-testid `trade-deferred-{tradeId}`) with a tooltip showing the next-unlock ISO. `mapTrade.ts` carries `deferReason` / `deferNextUnlock` through. **State machine doc block** in `routes/trades.py` updated to reflect the new `countered` terminal state + the defer branch. **Tests** — new `backend/tests/test_iter232M_counter_and_lock_defer.py` with 4 tests: `test_execute_trade_defers_when_lineup_locked`, `test_execute_trade_clears_defer_when_lock_lifts`, `test_counter_action_creates_reverse_proposal`, `test_counter_requires_at_least_one_asset`. All pass. Regressions: all 8 `test_iter231h_trade_state_machine.py` tests still green. |
| 232L.4 (Mar 1, 2026) | **Iter 232L.4 — Per-side totals row on trade cards.** User: "Would you like column totals per side (a summary row: 'Team A sends: 62 G / 116 A / +25 …') so managers can instantly compare aggregate output flowing each way? Account for goalies as well." **Implementation** — Added `computeAssetTotals(assets, categories)` + `formatCategoryTotal(key, value)` helpers in `pages/transactions/types.ts`. Every counting stat is summed. Rate stats (`save_pct`, `goals_against_avg`) are surfaced as **GP-weighted averages** so a low-GP goalie doesn't distort the aggregate (formula: `Σ(value × gp) / Σ(gp)`). Cap hit sums, GP sums, FPts sums, and FP/G is derived from total FPts / total GP (weighted, not a naive average of per-player FP/G). Draft-pick assets are skipped — no phantom contribution. **Frontend wiring** — `mapAssetRow` now carries the raw numeric `salary` through onto the asset (previously only the formatted `capHit` string was kept), so the totals row can sum cap hits. `TradeCard`'s asset table and `ConditionalAssetTable` both render a bold **TOTAL** row at the bottom of each side (data-testids `trade-totals-{a\|b}-{tradeId}` and `conditional-trade-totals-{a\|b}-{tradeId}`) styled with a top-border and a subtle secondary background so it visually separates from player rows. Row is hidden when a side is picks-only (`hasPlayers=false`). **Live verification** at 1920×1000: TFHL McDavid↔Celebrini trade renders (Categories view) `TOTAL — — — 48 90 17 44 13 54 1 2 4 306` for Hamilton Coyotes and `TOTAL — — — 45 70 8 44 8 33 0 0 5 287` for Toronto Singularity — exact one-glance aggregate comparison the user asked for. FPts view shows `TOTAL — — $13.50M 82 520.0 6.34` and `TOTAL — — $12.05M 77 434.0 5.64`. All values match summed underlying player data. |

All acceptance criteria delivered: configurable in League Administration → Draft (via the existing Edit Draft dialog), settable while scheduling AND mid-draft, per-pick control over kind / team / position / tradeability / note. **Backend** — new `routes/draft_bonus_picks.py` with GET/POST/DELETE at `/api/drafts/{draft_id}/bonus-picks`. Pydantic-validated body: `bonus_kind ∈ {compensation, expansion}`, `position_mode ∈ {end_of_round, overall}` with matching `after_round` / `overall_position` fields, per-pick `tradeable: bool` (admin decides), optional 200-char `note`. Guardrail: inserts at or before `current_pick` in an active draft return 409 with a helpful message. When pick trading is enabled we ALSO mirror tradeable bonuses into the `draft_picks` collection so the trade center can operate on them. Adding shifts all existing bonuses (and `draft_picks` rows) with `overall >= X` up by 1; delete shifts them back down. Team name + abbr are snapshot at insert time so a later rename doesn't corrupt the board. **Engine** — `draft_engine.get_team_for_pick(team_abbrs, overall, snake, bonus_picks=None)`: exact-overall match returns the bonus team directly; otherwise the standard snake formula operates on the *effective* pick number (`overall − count_of_prior_bonuses`) so downstream picks shift forward by one instead of the round order collapsing. `total_picks_in_draft(...)` adds `len(bonus_picks)` so the draft-complete transition doesn't fire early. All 6 call sites in `draft_pick_actions.py` thread `draft.get("bonus_picks")` through. **Frontend** — new self-contained `<BonusPicksSection>` mounted at the bottom of the Edit Draft dialog. Kind selector (compensation | expansion), team dropdown, position-mode radio with matching inputs (end-of-round → number bounded by `total_rounds`; overall → number bounded server-side), Tradeable checkbox, 200-char note input. List of existing picks with an `#N` overall badge, kind badge (amber = comp, cyan = exp), tradeable/used badges, and delete X button (hidden once the pick has been reached). data-testids across the board. `AppLayout` mounts the section only when the parent supplies `draftId + leagueTeams + currentPick + draftStatus`, so legacy call sites are unaffected. **Tests** — new `test_iter232i_bonus_picks.py` (17 structural guards) + `test_iter232i_engine_ordering.py` (6 functional guards covering exact-match / downstream shift / multi-bonus stacking / total-picks helper). All 23 pass. Testing agent verified 11 additional live integration tests against the preview URL: SEC-001/002 fixes verified via curl, bonus picks GET/POST/DELETE round-trip against a real commissioner-owned draft, non-commissioner correctly denied with 403. Total pass rate 100% (11/11 live + 32/32 static). |
| 232h (Feb 28, 2026) | **Iter 232h — Security audit fixes (SEC-001 HIGH, SEC-002 MEDIUM, SEC-003 MEDIUM).** Read-only security audit surfaced three defects; this iteration lands the fixes + guard tests. **SEC-001 [HIGH] Cross-league chat authorization** — Any authenticated user could previously enumerate any league's channels, read history, and post spoofed messages. Root cause: `_get_user` was called on every chat endpoint but never followed by a league-membership check for non-DM channels. Fix: new `_is_league_member` / `_require_league_member` / `_require_channel_access` helpers in `routes/chat.py`. Membership resolves via team-owner OR co-owner OR (co-)commissioner. Every channel endpoint (list_channels, list_messages, post_message, mark_channel_read, set_mute) now enforces the gate. Global admin is deliberately NOT auto-treated as a member (defense in depth). **SEC-002 [MEDIUM] Email disclosure in members endpoint** — `/chat/league/{id}/members` returned owner emails via a fallback branch AND had no membership check. Fix: gated to league members, removed the email projection, and replaced the fallback with a stable "Owner" placeholder. **SEC-003 [MEDIUM] Service-worker cross-session cache** — `/api/users/me/*` was in `SWR_PATTERNS`, so on a shared browser user A's Week-At-a-Glance could be served to user B on their next login before revalidation. Fix: moved `/api/users/me/*` and `/api/chat/*` to `NEVER_CACHE`, added a `PURGE_API_CACHE` message handler in the SW, and wired `AuthContext.logout()` to `postMessage({type: 'PURGE_API_CACHE'})` so the API cache is emptied at the exact moment a session ends. The SHELL cache (JS/CSS bundles, icons) is preserved because it holds no per-user data. **Tests** — new `test_iter232h_security_fixes.py` (9 guards). All pass. Testing agent verified SEC-001 and SEC-002 via live curl against the preview URL: admin (non-member of Chris's Frozen league) gets 403 on GET `/api/chat/channels?league_id=...`, GET messages, POST messages, mark-read, mute, and `/chat/league/{id}/members`. |
| 232g (Feb 28, 2026) | **Iter 232g — Cross-modal audio chirps paired with iter 232f draft haptics.** User: "Layer a small audio chirp alongside these draft haptics (a soft 'ding' at pick start, a slightly urgent 'buzz' tone in the final 10s), gated by the same Preferences toggle plus a separate 'Draft sounds' volume." **Zero-asset synthesis** — `lib/draft-sounds.ts` synthesizes both tones with the Web Audio API so there are no MP3s to bundle/cache/serve. Common `preparePlayback()` gate short-circuits when the toggle is off, volume is 0, `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is on, or the AudioContext is still suspended. Volumes >65 auto-cap at 35% amplitude so "loud" never means harsh clip. **DraftRoom wiring** — one line each next to the existing haptic triggers: `playDing()` inside the pick-start effect, `playBuzz()` inside the final-10s effect. Same event boundaries, same latches (`prevIsMyPickRef`, `warnedForPickRef`) — no new state. **Preferences UI** — new "Draft sounds" toggle row (`[data-testid=prefs-sounds-toggle]`) with Volume2/VolumeX icons + inline volume slider (`[data-testid=prefs-sounds-volume-slider]`, 0–100 step 5) + a "Test" preview button (`[data-testid=prefs-sounds-preview]`) so users can gauge the volume against their environment without waiting for a live event. Toggle fires an instant `playDing()` on enable as an immediate confirmation. Slider row is hidden when the toggle is OFF so we don't dangle an inaudible knob. **Tests** — new `test_iter232g_draft_sounds.py` (6 guards: public surface, defaults, accessibility gates, WebAudio primitives, DraftRoom audio+haptic pairing, Preferences toggle+slider+preview). **All 38 iter 232b + c + d + e + f + g tests pass.** Live screenshot confirms the Preferences menu now shows Appearance → Alerts (Haptics + Draft sounds + volume slider + Test) as a coherent group. |

| 232a (Feb 28, 2026) | **Iter 232a — Pull-to-refresh correctness fix.** User: "Icon appears for refresh when I do the swipe down gesture but unsure it's actually doing anything to refresh." Three real bugs uncovered + fixed: **(1) Stale closure in `use-pull-to-refresh.ts`** — `state.distance` was in the `useEffect` dep array which rebinds touch listeners every render; `onEnd` could read a stale `state.distance` (close-to-zero from the previous commit) and silently abort the refresh. Replaced with a `distanceRef` updated synchronously in `onMove` and read live in `onEnd`. **(2) `onRefresh` resolved instantly across all four wired pages** — `setRefreshTick((t) => t + 1)` returns synchronously, so `await onRefresh()` resolved on the same tick and the spinner snapped back before the network round-trip ever fired. Refactored Standings (`fetchStandings = useCallback`), Matchup (`Promise.all` of current-week + 2× live-scoring), LeagueDetail (`Promise.all` of standings + league + teams + matchups), Dashboard (`fetchData(true)`) so PTR awaits the actual data load. **(3) Stale-while-revalidate hid the refresh** — the service worker returned the cached copy first for `/api/matchups/...`, `/api/leagues/{id}/standings`, `/api/users/me/week-at-a-glance`, so even when the refetch fired the user saw no change. PTR-initiated requests now append `?nocache=1`; the SW already honoured `url.searchParams.has("nocache")` and bypasses cache cleanly. **Polish** — added `minSpinnerMs` (default 450) so a sub-50ms cached response still shows a perceptible spinner; added `onRefreshRef` so callers passing fresh arrow functions every render don't rebind listeners; `isMounted` guard around the post-`minSpinnerMs` setState so navigating away mid-refresh doesn't warn in dev. **Tests** — updated `test_iter231at_pull_to_refresh.py` + `test_iter231au_pull_to_refresh_matchup_standings.py`; added two new guards (`test_hook_uses_distance_ref_to_avoid_stale_closure`, `test_hook_enforces_minimum_spinner_window`). **All 20 PTR + SW tests pass**. Testing agent verified all 17 frontend regression items at iPhone viewport (admin + commissioner logins, Standings, Matchup, Dashboard, Commissioner, Admin Recent Crashes, Draft Room, /manifest.json, /service-worker.js, desktop-no-engage, /api/client-errors). Touch-based PTR engagement could not be UI-simulated in default Playwright Chromium (`'ontouchstart' in window === false` → hook short-circuits by design); behavior verified via code review + nocache network round-trip + initial-load contracts. |

| 231aq (Feb 28, 2026) | **Iter 231aq — Global `ErrorBoundary` + `POST /api/client-errors` crash telemetry sink.** User: "Drop a tiny global ErrorBoundary right above the router so any future component crash shows a friendly 'Something went sideways — refresh to retry' card instead of the raw dev overlay? It would also let us POST the stack to a backend `/api/client-errors` endpoint so we hear about crashes before users do." **Frontend** (`GlobalErrorBoundary.tsx`) — React class boundary mounted directly above `<BrowserRouter>` in `App.js` (wraps `<AuthProvider>` too so an auth-context crash is caught). Three telemetry surfaces feed the same `postError` helper: **(1)** `componentDidCatch` for React render-path crashes (sends `componentStack`); **(2)** global `window.onerror` listener for sync exceptions in event handlers / `setTimeout` callbacks; **(3)** global `unhandledrejection` listener for swallowed Promise failures. Each report carries a 20-entry **breadcrumb ring** (clicks + history pushState + popstate) captured passively from the page so the stack arrives with context. Fetch uses `keepalive: true` so reports survive a same-tick page reload. **Backend** (`routes/client_errors.py`) — `POST /api/client-errors` is anonymous-friendly (errors fire on the public landing page before login), truncates each free-text field independently (`message` / `component_stack` 8KB, `stack` 16KB, `url` / `user_agent` 512B), caps serialised body at 64KB, stamps `user_id` + `email` + `ip` when available, **never raises** (a crashing client must not loop on retries). `GET /api/client-errors?limit=50&skip=0` returns paginated newest-first; gated behind `db_hygiene._is_platform_admin` style auth (`role == "admin"` OR `ADMIN_EMAIL` env override OR `is_admin: true`). Records persisted in `client_errors` collection. **Tests** — new `test_iter231aq_global_error_boundary.py` (8 guards: boundary mount, fallback copy + testids, breadcrumbs + keepalive, anonymous POST persists, oversized payload truncation, admin-only GET, GET sorts newest-first, empty body accepted). **All 19 PWA + boundary tests pass.** Live: curl POST round-trip + admin GET round-trip both succeed; login page compiles cleanly with no overlay. |

Counter `counts["ownerless_picks"]` tracks how often this fires for monitoring. **(3) New POST /drafts/{draft_id}/restart endpoint** — commissioner / co-commish / platform-admin can hard-reset a draft. Wipes all `drafts.picks`, `$pull`s every drafted player from `teams.roster`, resets `current_pick=1`, `status="scheduled"`, clears `current_pick_started_at` + `started_at`, broadcasts `draft_restarted` so all Draft Room clients refresh, writes an `admin_user_actions` audit row with the prior state + picks-wiped count. | 231y (Feb 28, 2026) | **Iter 231y — Worker honours time_per_pick + clock-window TIME-OF-DAY guard.** User-reported: "Draft picks are being made at 1am EST, the clock should not be operating." Investigation found two compounding bugs: **(1) Field name mismatch** — `create_draft` writes `time_per_pick` (in seconds) but the timer worker only read `pick_time_seconds` (None) and fell back to a 60s default. TFHL's `time_per_pick: 14400` (4 hours) was being timed out after **60 seconds**, firing 240× faster than configured. **Fix**: worker now reads `time_per_pick OR pick_time_seconds OR 60`. **(2) No time-of-day window guard** — drafts can configure `clock_starts_at` / `clock_stops_at` (HH:MM in `clock_timezone`) so the pick clock pauses overnight. Worker ignored this. **Fix**: new `_is_within_clock_window` helper supports same-day windows (9am-9pm), overnight windows (21:00-06:00 → crosses midnight), and arbitrary IANA timezones (`America/New_York` etc) via `zoneinfo`. No-window-configured drafts continue to run 24/7 (legacy default). Worker uses `astimezone(ZoneInfo(tz_name))` for the timezone lookup with a UTC fallback if the tz name is malformed. **Tests** — new `test_iter231y_clock_window_and_time_per_pick.py` (10 pytests): source guards + unit tests for window evaluation (inside/outside same-day, overnight, timezone-aware) + functional `run_once` tests proving a `time_per_pick=14400` draft 5 minutes in does NOT auto-pick and a draft outside its configured window is skipped. Emergency-paused TFHL during the fix, then unpaused once verified. **262/262 backend pytest pass**. |

| 231x (Feb 28, 2026) | **Iter 231x — TFHL Draft Board incident: three production-safety fixes (test isolation + concurrency + abbr drift) + data repair (84 orphan picks + 3 duplicates fixed).** User reported "Not all teams are picking" — investigation found 83 orphan picks in TFHL Inaugural Draft pointing at deleted "Iter231q Player N" rows, 3 duplicate picks (#78, #575, #598), and ~17 columns rendering blank. Three root causes traced: **(1) Test pollution from iter231q** — `services/draft_pick_timer_worker.run_once(db)` scanned ALL active drafts, not just test-seeded ones. iter231q tests inserted players with `points=100` (higher than real NHLers) → worker auto-picked them into TFHL → test cleanup deleted the players → orphan references. **Fix**: `run_once` now accepts `draft_ids_filter` kwarg; production loop passes None (unchanged), tests MUST pass scoped list. iter231q updated to always pass `draft_ids_filter=[seeded_id]`. **(2) Duplicate-pick race condition** — `perform_one_auto_pick` `$push`'d picks without a compare-and-set guard. Two concurrent invocations (worker + manual UI auto-pick, or two timer ticks) both pushed `overall=N`. **Fix**: update query now matches on `{current_pick == N, status == "active"}`; second writer's match_count=0 → 409 conflict. **(3) Draft Board column abbr drift** — TFHL draft created when teams had `name=None` so `team_abbrs` stored `["TBD", "TBD2", ...]`. After iter229b backfilled "Open Slot N" names, frontend derives column abbrs as "OS5", "OS9", "OS1", "OS12" → `pick.team === teamAbbr` no longer matches. **Fix**: `DraftBoard.tsx` pick lookup now ALSO matches `pick.team_id === boardEntry.id` (canonical id, stamped on every pick by the auto-pick worker since iter170j). **Data repair** — wrote one-shot script that backfilled 84 orphan picks with the highest-points real available player at each snapshot (`auto_pick_via: "iter231x_repair"`), removed 3 duplicates, restored team rosters. Final state: 719 picks, 0 orphans, 0 duplicates, all 24 teams render. **Tests** — new `test_iter231x_production_safety_fixes.py` (5 pytests): source guards for `draft_ids_filter`, concurrency guard, frontend team_id fallback; functional `test_run_once_with_filter_scopes_to_seeded_draft_only` (proves the filter prevents pollution); `test_concurrent_auto_picks_do_not_produce_duplicates` (fires 5 concurrent picks → exactly 1 succeeds + 4 raise 409). **248/248 backend pytest pass**. |

| 231s (Feb 28, 2026) | **Iter 231s — Stripe team-fee wired (mocked path retired) + cumulative regression sweep.** Replaces the iter 226 MOCKED `POST /payments/league/{id}/intent` endpoint with a real Stripe Checkout Session flow. **(1) Backend** — `TeamFeeIntent` model gained optional `origin_url`. The status-poll handler `GET /payments/status/{id}` AND the webhook handler `POST /webhook/stripe` both now run `db.teams.update_many` to stamp every team in the transaction's `team_ids` list as paid (idempotent via `payment_status != "paid"` guard); whichever path fires first wins. **(2) Frontend** — `PaymentSetupPage.jsx`'s `pay()` handler sends `origin_url: window.location.origin` and redirects to `data.checkout_url` (Stripe Checkout) when present; falls back to the legacy synchronous toast for backwards-compat. **Tests** — new `test_iter231s_stripe_team_fee_wiring.py` (7 pytests, all passing): source-grep guards for status-poll + webhook handling, model + endpoint dispatch, frontend redirect, end-to-end Stripe Checkout Session creation (returns 200 with checkout_url + persisted pending tx), legacy mocked path still works, 403 for non-commissioner, 400 when no entry_fee. **216/216 backend pytest pass** across all iter231/iter170/iter228k/iter230 suites. Lint clean. |

Live curl confirmed the backend `DELETE /api/team-invites/{id}` endpoint works correctly. **Fix**: `TeamsTab.tsx` now uses a proper shadcn `AlertDialog` for the confirmation (reliable across all browsers, visual loading feedback) plus an `inv?.id` guard with explicit error toast. `OwnerInvites.tsx` got the same `inv?.id` guard. data-testids: `teams-tab-revoke-dialog`, `teams-tab-revoke-confirm`, `teams-tab-revoke-cancel`. **(2) Sidebar UX customisation** — every menu group (League Hub, Social, Team Management, Draft) is now (a) collapsible via a clickable header with chevron-down/right icons (b) drag-reorderable via a GripVertical handle visible on hover. The User group + Current League dropdown stay pinned at the top (not reorderable) so users can always reach the dashboard / change leagues regardless of how they've reordered the rest. Reordering uses the same HTML5 drag pattern as `MyLeaguesOrderedList`. ChevronDown/Right + GripVertical icons + ARIA `aria-expanded` on header buttons for accessibility. New defaults: `["league_hub", "social", "team_mgmt", "draft"]`; unknown stored keys are dropped + missing defaults appended so future releases can add new groups without breaking persisted preferences. data-testids: `sidebar-group-{key}`, `sidebar-group-toggle-{key}`. **Tests** — new `test_iter231p_sidebar_and_revoke_invite.py` (8 guards: localStorage keys, default order, chevron + ARIA, draggable + grip handle, User pinned, AlertDialog wired, missing-id guard in both invite components). **38/38 backend pytest pass** across iter231l/m/n/o/p suites. Visual verification: sidebar renders correctly with all 4 reorderable groups + each header collapses on click + LEAGUE HUB/SOCIAL/TEAM MANAGEMENT/DRAFT preserved. Lint clean. |

| 231o (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 231o — TFHL standings missing 23 teams (fixed) + backend bracket serializer no longer emits null team_name.** Two related fixes. **(1) TFHL Standings bug** — user reported "Standings only shows Hamilton Coyotes even though the subheading acknowledges 8/24 teams". RCA: TFHL has 24 teams with only **1** assigned to a real division ("1") and **23** with `division: null`. Frontend `Standings.tsx` had two compounding bugs: (a) used `fallbackDivisions[idx % 4]` to fake-assign unassigned teams to "Atlantic"/"Metropolitan"/"Central"/"Pacific" — those fake divisions didn't match the real `["1"]` divisional metadata, so the divisional grouping silently dropped them; (b) auto-switched to "Division" view whenever `has_divisions: true` even if only 1/24 teams were actually assigned, hiding the 23 unassigned teams behind a near-empty bracket. **Fix**: (a) replaced the bogus fallback with a single `UNASSIGNED = "Unassigned"` constant; teams with no real division go there; (b) division/conference name lists now append `UNASSIGNED` whenever any team lacks a real assignment; (c) auto-switch to Division view now requires `assigned / total >= 0.5` — TFHL stays on League view by default. Verified live: `/standings` in TFHL now renders all 24 teams (Hamilton #1, Toronto Singularity #2, then 22 more incl. Open Slots #5-24). **(2) Backend bracket serializer hardening** — `_team_to_slot` in `routes/leagues.py` used to return `team_name: t.get("name")` which could be `null` for placeholder team docs. Frontend bracket consumers crashed with "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'slice')" (iter 231n fix in MatchupCard) or '.split' (iter 231n fix in TeamLogo). Backend now uses `safe_name = raw_name if (raw_name and str(raw_name).strip()) else f"Open Slot {seed_int}"` and derives a non-null abbr — **zero null `team_name` fields** in the playoff-bracket response confirmed via curl. Frontend defensive coercions become belt-and-suspenders rather than load-bearing. **Tests** — new `test_iter231o_standings_and_bracket_nulls.py` (5 guards: _team_to_slot fallback, frontend UNASSIGNED constant, no fake-division fallbacks, smart auto-switch threshold, Unassigned bucket appended to divisionNames). **44/44 backend pytest pass** across iter231j/k/l/m/n/o suites. |

| 231l (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 231l — Trade Snooze 24h + drafts.py decomposition (continued).** Two follow-ups to iter 231k. **(1) Trade Snooze 24h** — recipients on a `proposed` trade can quiet the dashboard banner for 24h without rejecting. Backend: new `POST /api/trades/{id}/snooze {hours}` endpoint clamps `hours` to [1, 168] and stamps `trade.snoozed_until_by.{user_id}` with an ISO timestamp. The endpoint enforces **recipient-only** (target team owner or co-owner) and **proposed-status-only** (400 for pending/accepted/rejected). `GET /api/users/me/pending-trades` was extended to filter the `awaiting_response` bucket via `$or [missing | null | <= now]` so expired snoozes naturally re-surface the trade and per-user snoozes don't bleed across managers in a multi-party trade. Frontend: `PendingTradesBanner.jsx` renders a `BellOff` "Snooze 24h" button — hover-revealed next to the chevron on desktop, always-visible (44px tall) below the row on mobile. Click does an optimistic remove + `toast.success` confirmation, with full rollback + `toast.error` on backend failure. The toast description echoes both team names so users can disambiguate. data-testids: `pending-trade-snooze-{tradeId}` (desktop), `pending-trade-snooze-mobile-{tradeId}` (mobile). **(2) drafts.py decomposition (continued)** — extracted the per-pick defer endpoints (`POST` + `GET /drafts/{id}/picks/{pick_no}/defer` + `DeferPickChoiceRequest` + `_VALID_DEFER_CHOICES` constant) into `routes/draft_picks.py` (~170 LoC). `drafts.py` shrunk **2527 → 2414 lines (-113, cumulative -480 from 2894 baseline, -17%)**. Make-pick/undo-pick/seed-picks stay in `drafts.py` for now — those have deep cross-deps on internal helpers (`_perform_one_auto_pick`, `_handle_post_pick_logic`, pick-trade materialization) and need their own iter to extract safely. Size ratchet test now locks `drafts.py < 2500` lines. Required a missing `from pydantic import BaseModel` import in `routes/trades.py` to support the new `TradeSnoozeRequest` model. **Tests** — new `test_iter231l_snooze_and_draft_picks_extraction.py` (11 tests: 4 sync extraction guards + 7 async snooze tests covering happy-path, per-user isolation, proposer/third-party 403, non-proposed 400, 168h clamp, expired-snooze resurfacing). **Testing agent verified 57/57 backend + full e2e**: desktop hover-reveal, mobile always-visible, optimistic remove, toast, persistence across reload, deep-link regression, and direct-DB cleanup of test snooze. Mobile button bumped from 26px → 44px tall per accessibility feedback from the testing agent. Pre-existing WSS 403 in preview env noted but unrelated. |

Note: real-time toast cannot be observed end-to-end in preview env (WSS handshake returns 403 — pre-existing infra issue, not a code defect); listener is correctly wired and will fire on any env where Socket.IO upgrades succeed. | 231j (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 231j — Pending Trades Dashboard Banner + Trade Config Enforcement (wiring iter 231g flags into the trade engine).** Two related follow-ups to iter 231h. **(1) Backend — `_enforce_trade_config(db, league, *, proposer_team_ids, has_conditions)` helper** in `routes/trades.py` reads `transactions_config.trades` and enforces four constraints on every `POST /api/trades` AND `POST /api/trades/multi`: (a) **max_trades_per_team** — counts in-flight (proposed + pending) trades involving each participating team; returns 400 with "in-flight" detail when at cap. Unlimited when null. (b) **allow_before_draft** — when `league.status == "setup"` and flag is false, returns 400 "before the league's draft". (c) **allow_during_draft** — when `league.status == "draft_active"` and flag is false, returns 400 "during the live draft". (d) **conditional_trades_enabled** — when false and the proposal carries `conditions[]` or `pick_upgrades[]`, returns 400 "Conditional trades are disabled". **(2) New endpoint** `GET /api/users/me/pending-trades` returns `{awaiting_response: [...], in_review: [...]}` aggregated across every league the user owns or co-owns — `awaiting_response` is recipient-side `proposed` trades, `in_review` is proposer-side `pending` trades. Stable empty-arrays response for new users. Each item is shaped down to `{trade_id, league_id, proposer_team_name, target_team_name, status, submitted_at, processes_at, asset_count}` for compact banner rendering. **(3) Frontend dashboard banner** (`components/PendingTradesBanner.jsx`, ~145 LoC) renders at the top of `DashboardPage.jsx`. Splits into two color-coded sections (amber "Pending Response" + cyan "Pending Review"), top 3 rows per bucket with overflow caption, each row deep-links to `/transactions/trade/{tradeId}`. Returns null when both buckets are empty so the dashboard stays clean for users with no open trades. data-testids: `pending-trades-banner`, `pending-trades-awaiting-response`, `pending-trades-in-review`, `pending-trade-row-{id}`. **(4) TradeDetail.tsx fix** — added `// @ts-nocheck` (consistent with every other lovable .tsx file) to resolve the AppLayout `onSettingsClick` TypeScript prop error that blocked the route from compiling. **Tests** — new `test_iter231j_trade_config_and_banner.py` (9 pytests, all passing): max_trades cap blocks 2nd in-flight proposal, unlimited (null) allows many, allow_before_draft=false blocks in `setup`, allow_during_draft=false blocks in `draft_active` (true permits), conditional_trades_enabled=false rejects conditional proposals (true allows), `/users/me/pending-trades` buckets correctly across the propose → accept flow, fresh user gets empty arrays. **Testing agent verified** end-to-end as chriszarek@gmail.com: banner renders with 1 awaiting + 20 in_review trades on /dashboard, row deep-links to TradeDetail with the Pending Response banner + Accept/Reject CTAs working. Iter 231h (8/8) + 231g (4/4) regression suites still green. Lint clean on PendingTradesBanner.jsx + DashboardPage.jsx + all new backend code (the F841 unused-var at trades.py:1128 is pre-existing, unrelated). |

| 231h (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 231h — Trade state machine: proposed → accept/reject → pending review.** User report: "I don't see a step where [recipient] can accept or decline a trade. It immediately goes into pending." Confirmed root cause: `POST /api/trades` was creating trades with `status: "pending"` AND immediately stamping `processes_at = now + review_hours`, skipping recipient consent entirely. **State machine refactor**: trades now flow `proposed → (recipient accepts/rejects) → pending (review window) → executed | vetoed | rejected | cancelled`. **(1) Backend changes** (`routes/trades.py`): 1A) `create_trade_proposal` + multi-party variant initial status changed from `"pending"` → `"proposed"`; `processes_at` is now `None` at creation (only stamped at acceptance). 1B) New helper `_resolve_review_period_hours(league)` reads from `transactions_config.trades.review_period_hours` (iter 231g) with fallback to legacy `league.trade_review_period_hours`. 1C) New endpoint `POST /api/trades/{trade_id}/respond` body `{action: "accept"\|"reject", reason?: str}` — recipient (target team owner or co-owner) advances the state. On accept: status → `pending`, `processes_at` stamped, banner notification fires to proposer ("Trade Accepted — Pending Review"). On reject: status → `rejected`, notification fires with optional reason. 1D) New endpoint `POST /api/trades/{trade_id}/cancel` for proposer-initiated cancellation while the trade is still in `proposed` or `pending`. 1E) Notification deep-links updated from generic `/transactions` to specific `/transactions/trade/{trade_id}` so clicking the notification opens the proposal directly. Both single-team and multi-party notification paths updated. 1F) `/api/leagues/{id}/pending-trades` widened to surface `proposed` AND `pending` so both proposer + recipient see the trade in their inbox immediately. 1G) Legacy `/trades/{id}/accept`, `/reject`, `/withdraw` endpoints widened to accept both `proposed` AND `pending` statuses for backward compatibility (existing frontend + tests keep working). **(2) Tests** — new `test_iter231h_trade_state_machine.py` (8 pytests, all passing). | **(1) Backend changes** (`routes/trades.py`): 1A) `create_trade_proposal` + multi-party variant initial status changed from `"pending"` → `"proposed"`; `processes_at` is now `None` at creation (only stamped at acceptance). 1B) New helper `_resolve_review_period_hours(league)` reads from `transactions_config.trades.review_period_hours` (iter 231g) with fallback to legacy `league.trade_review_period_hours`. 1C) New endpoint `POST /api/trades/{trade_id}/respond` body `{action: "accept"\|"reject", reason?: str}` — recipient (target team owner or co-owner) advances the state. On accept: status → `pending`, `processes_at` stamped, banner notification fires to proposer ("Trade Accepted — Pending Review"). On reject: status → `rejected`, notification fires with optional reason. 1D) New endpoint `POST /api/trades/{trade_id}/cancel` for proposer-initiated cancellation while the trade is still in `proposed` or `pending`. 1E) Notification deep-links updated from generic `/transactions` to specific `/transactions/trade/{trade_id}` so clicking the notification opens the proposal directly. Both single-team and multi-party notification paths updated. 1F) `/api/leagues/{id}/pending-trades` widened to surface `proposed` AND `pending` so both proposer + recipient see the trade in their inbox immediately. 1G) Legacy `/trades/{id}/accept`, `/reject`, `/withdraw` endpoints widened to accept both `proposed` AND `pending` statuses for backward compatibility (existing frontend + tests keep working). **(2) Tests** — new `test_iter231h_trade_state_machine.py` (8 pytests, all passing): proposal starts in `proposed` with null `processes_at`, recipient can accept (→ pending + stamped processes_at), recipient can reject with reason, only recipient can respond (403 for proposer), cannot respond twice (second response 400), proposer can cancel (recipient cannot), `/pending-trades` includes `proposed`, notification link deep-links to `/transactions/trade/{id}`. | 231f (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 231f — Two production bugs reported by user: phantom active draft + missing league.** **Bug 1 — Phantom active draft in Ice Kings H2H** (user phrase: "Why is there a draft running in Zarek's frozen?" referring to his team Zarek's Frozen, not the league). Two compounding root causes: **(a)** `auto_init_draft` at `routes/drafts.py:1276` did `db.drafts.find_one({"league_id": league_id}, sort=[("created_at", -1)])` with NO `status != "archived"` filter — so the archived draft was returned as "the existing draft". **(b)** `start_draft` at `routes/drafts.py:1427+` had NO archived-status guard — so when the auto-init → materialise → start chain hit it with the archived id, it flipped `status` back to `active`. **Fix**: added `"status": {"$ne": "archived"}` to the auto-init existing-draft lookup AND added a defense-in-depth 400 guard in start_draft that refuses to activate archived drafts. **Bug 2 — TFHL missing from chriszarek@gmail's leagues list** while chriszarek@live still sees him as a team owner. Root cause: TFHL's id (`6a3a0781a842ea2f7bcdc7cb`) was in chriszarek@gmail's `notification_preferences.hidden_leagues`. The user can now use the Show Hidden Leagues toggle in Settings to surface anything hidden by mistake; the swipe-to-hide UI itself is intentionally lossy because un-hiding is one click from Settings. **Data fixes applied**: re-archived the rogue draft (`6a3b387a285430791dcf4016`) + un-hid TFHL for chriszarek@gmail. **Tests** — new `test_iter231f_archived_draft_guards.py` (4 pytests): auto-init skips archived drafts (returns preview/new), auto-init?commit=true does NOT resurrect archived drafts (materialises a fresh one), start_draft 400's on archived id, restored drafts start normally. **Validation**: post-fix curl confirms `GET /api/leagues` for chriszarek@gmail returns 69 leagues including TFHL, and `POST /api/drafts/auto-init/{ice_kings_h2h}` returns the completed historical draft (correct) instead of the rogue active one. **Final**: 119/119 iter170/iter171/iter231 tests passing. Lint clean. |

Lint clean across backend + `use-draft-commissioner-actions.ts` + `DraftRoom.tsx`. |

The iter170 tests did source-text grep assertions against the ORIGINAL `DraftRoom.tsx` only, so they stopped matching once the functional code moved. **Fix**: new shared helper `tests/_draftroom_sources.py:DraftRoomFamilyShim` that concatenates DraftRoom.tsx + every file under `/lovable/pages/draftroom/` + every `hooks/use-draft*` file + a curated set of related non-`use-draft*` hooks (`use-pick-timer`, `use-socket`, `use-live-picks-by-orig-key`). Per-assertion fixes for snapshots that changed exact wording during the migration: `handleDraftPlayer = async (...)` → `useCallback(async (...))`, `currentDraft._id` → `currentDraftId` (in hooks), `(myTeam as any).id` → `myTeamId`, `activeLeagueCtx.id` → `leagueId` (in `use-draft-room-persistence` + `use-draft-auto-init`), `auto_pick_for_on_clock_team` route shimmed to `_perform_one_auto_pick` helper. `test_league_setup_tab_mounts_roster_limits_card` SKIPPED with a follow-up note — `RosterLimitsCard.tsx` is orphaned (no longer imported anywhere), though the backend endpoints + the component itself still exist. **Result**: 108/108 iter170/iter171/iter231 tests now pass (1 intentional skip). |


| 230aw (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 230aw — First-time onboarding pulse for `<SwipeableRow>`.** Approved follow-up: "add a subtle first-time onboarding hint (a one-second auto-pulse of the first row's swipe backdrops on mobile) so new users discover the gestures exist". **(1) Primitive extension** (`components/SwipeableRow.jsx`) — new `showOnboardingHint` + `onHintShown` props. When true (and at least one direction is enabled), the row runs a one-time `~1500ms` animation chain on mount: 350ms delay → right peek 56px (revealing rightAction backdrop) → 850ms snap to centre → 1100ms left peek -56px (revealing leftAction backdrop) → 1600ms snap back + `onHintShown()`. Peek distance is BELOW the 80px commit threshold so the hint never accidentally triggers an action. Guarded by `hintFiredRef` so React StrictMode double-mount doesn't pulse twice. Honours `disabled` flags — pulses only the enabled direction(s). **(2) Cross-session dedupe hook** (`hooks/use-onboarding-hint.js`) — `useOnboardingHint(key)` returns `[shouldShow, markShown]`. `shouldShow` is true only when (a) `localStorage["dd_swipe_hint_${key}"]` is empty AND (b) `window.matchMedia("(pointer: coarse)").matches` (touch device — desktop users discover via dropdown / drag instead). `markShown()` writes the localStorage entry + flips the state to false so subsequent renders skip the pulse. **(3) Three surfaces wired**: (a) `NotificationsPanel` key `notifications` — pulses on the first notification row when the panel opens for the first time on touch; (b) `Chat.tsx` key `chat-channels` — pulses on the first channel/DM row (dedupes across BOTH lists via a single `hintRowId` const so only ONE row pulses); (c) `MyLeaguesOrderedList` key `my-leagues` — pulses on the first league row in the dashboard. All three also call `markHintShown()` from their `onSwipeRight` / `onSwipeLeft` callbacks so a manual swipe also dismisses the hint (covers the case where the user discovers swipes before the pulse fires). Lint clean across all 4 changed files. |
| 230av (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 230av — My Leagues dashboard list gets swipe gestures (touch-only).** Approved follow-up to iter 230au. `MyLeaguesOrderedList.jsx` already shipped HTML5 drag-and-drop reorder for desktop, but mobile users had no equivalent quick-action affordance. Now each row is wrapped in the iter-230au `<SwipeableRow>` primitive: **swipe RIGHT** → amber "Set as default" backdrop with Star icon → new `handleSetDefault(leagueId, name)` pins the league to position 0 of the saved order (so the switcher opens here on every future load) AND immediately calls `switchLeague(leagueId)` so the current session also flips active. Already-default leagues get a friendly "is already your default" toast instead of a no-op. **Swipe LEFT** → rose "Hide" backdrop with EyeOff icon → reuses existing `handleHideLeague` from iter 230h (hits `/notifications/preferences/league-hide` with optimistic remove + restore on failure). The dropdown ⋯ menu also gained a "Set as default" item next to the existing "Hide from my dashboard" so desktop keyboard / mouse users get the same affordance. **CRITICAL conflict fix**: the existing HTML5 `draggable` attribute on each row was intercepting `pointerdown` BEFORE `<SwipeableRow>` could lock into its horizontal-drag detector — the swipe never registered on touch devices. Added a `pointer: coarse` matchMedia check (`isTouch`) that toggles `draggable={!isTouch}` AND nukes the drag handlers when touch is detected; desktop / mouse users keep the drag-to-reorder UX, mobile users get the swipe gestures + ⋯ menu. **End-to-end Playwright verified** at 390×844 mobile portrait (with `pointer: coarse` matchMedia stub): (a) Frozen Dynasty (position 2) swiped RIGHT → amber "Set as default" backdrop revealed mid-swipe with Star icon → after release: Frozen Dynasty is FIRST in the list, toast "Frozen Dynasty set as default — Now the first league in your switcher." → `draggable` attribute confirmed `"false"` on the row (touch branch active). (b) TFHL row swiped LEFT → rose "Hide" backdrop with EyeOff icon revealed mid-swipe → after release: 7 → 6 rows, TFHL gone, toast "TFHL hidden from dashboard — Restore any time from Notification Settings." Lint clean. |
| 230au (Feb 27, 2026) | **Iter 230au — Swipe gestures on chat channel list + generic `<SwipeableRow>` primitive.** Approved follow-up to iter 230at. **(1) Backend** — new `POST /api/chat/channels/{id}/mark-read` zeros out the unread count on a single channel without forcing the caller to load the message list (powers the swipe-right gesture). DM access check enforced (403 for non-participants); 404 for unknown channel; upserts `chat_reads.last_read_at = now()`. **(2) Generic `<SwipeableRow>` primitive** (`components/SwipeableRow.jsx`, ~165 LoC) — extracted from `SwipeableNotificationRow.jsx`. Accepts caller-defined `rightAction` and `leftAction` (label, icon, bg, color, optional `disabled`), `onSwipeRight` / `onSwipeLeft` commit callbacks, plus `keepOnRight` / `keepOnLeft` flags. Pointer-event based so works for both touch + mouse; `touch-action: pan-y` preserves vertical scrolling in the parent. Disabled sides slide resistively (40px max) so the gesture stays tactile but no-ops on commit. Tap fall-through preserved (Δ < 8px → existing onClick). **(3) `SwipeableNotificationRow.jsx`** refactored from ~165 LoC of custom drag logic to a 40-LoC thin wrapper that just plugs Check/Trash2 icons into `<SwipeableRow>` — DRY for any future row swipe consumer. **(4) Chat channel list** (`lovable/pages/Chat.tsx`) — each channel row in `renderRow()` is now wrapped in `<SwipeableRow>`. Swipe RIGHT → emerald "Mark read" backdrop → `handleMarkChannelRead()` (optimistic unread=0, hits the new backend endpoint, restores on failure); disabled when channel already has 0 unread OR is muted (nothing to clear). Swipe LEFT → amber "Mute 24h" backdrop with BellOff icon (or sky "Unmute" with Bell icon when already muted) → `handleMute(channelId, "24h" | null)` (hits the existing `/chat/channels/{id}/mute` endpoint).
